- Why Expensive Products Feel Better: Kind: captions Language: en Why do expensive products feel better even when cheaper products do the exact same thing? The answer has less to do with quality and more to do with psychology. Brands spend billions engineering how products make you feel before you even touch them. Our brains naturally associate high price with high quality. When something is expensive, we assume it must be better. Even if two products are nearly identical, most people will perceive the expensive one as more premium. Luxury brands don't just sell products. They sell identity, emotion, and status. Owning expensive products often makes people feel more successful, respected, or powerful. That's why brands like Apple, Rolex, and Rolls-Royce focus heavily on image and exclusivity. People are not only buying the product, they are buying the feeling attached to it. Psychology also changes physical experience. Studies show that people often enjoy expensive products more simply because they believe they are premium. Expensive coffee can taste better. Expensive perfume can feel more luxurious. Even products with similar quality can feel completely different because expectations change perception. Every detail inside a premium brand is carefully designed. packaging, lighting, store layout, music, colors, even how employees speak. Companies like Apple and Starbucks understand that perception shapes behavior. The product is only part of the experience. The emotional atmosphere matters just as much. Humans like to believe they biologically, but most buying decisions are heavily influenced by emotion, identity, and perception. The world's biggest brands understand this extremely well. They don't just sell products. They shape how people feel.

- I Compared Expensive Vs Affordable Luxury (Louis Vuitton Vs Coach): Kind: captions Language: ru Я потратила 4450 долларов на эту сумку Louis Vuitton в Париже и 395 долларов на эту сумку Coach в Далласе. Когда я разобрала обе, качество изготовления оказалось практически идентичным. Качество кожи? Довольно близкое. Фурнитура? Вероятно, отличить их было бы невозможно. Так почему же одна стоит в 11 раз дороже другой? Я вложила более миллиона долларов собственных денег и времени в разборку люксовых сумок за последние четыре года. И это сравнение LV и Coach не давало мне покоя, потому что ответ не тот, о котором вы думаете. И как только вы это увидите, вы никогда больше не будете делать покупки так, как раньше. Я разобрала множество вещей от двух самых известных в мире брендов кожаных изделий: LV и Coach. Оба имеют глобальные сети магазинов и долгую историю. Coach — американский бренд, а LV — гордость французской роскоши. То, что я обнаружила между этими двумя гигантами, касалось не только качества. Речь шла о чем-то гораздо более дорогом в производстве и гораздо более ценном в продаже. Я купил и сравнил три комплекта аналогичных товаров от обеих марок, и вот что я обнаружил. Начнём с товаров начального уровня. Портфель из канваса с монограммой от LV, за который я заплатил 1700 долларов, и рюкзак из аутлета Coach, за который я заплатил меньше 150 долларов. Дизайн портфеля LV был слишком простым. Рюкзак Coach, напротив, имел гораздо более продуманные детали, которые делали сумку намного удобнее. Материалы канваса были похожи, но кожаная отделка, использованная в Coach, ощущалась намного лучше, чем та, что была на сумке LV. Фурнитура? Очень похожая. Но вот что меня шокировало. Портфель LV был деформирован прямо из коробки. Это произошло из-за неправильной сборки. Рюкзак Coach был безупречен, и мы пользуемся им уже четыре года. С точки зрения качества, здесь явно выигрывает Coach, поскольку я заплатил меньше 1/10 цены LV. Этот результат меня озадачил, но давайте продолжим. Далее, сумки-тоут. Я купила сумку Coach Theo в магазине в Фриско, штат Техас, за 450 долларов. Мне нравится практичность дизайна и качество кожи. Я спросила продавца о внутренней подкладке. Она выглядела как замша, но у меня были сомнения. Она дважды заверила меня, что это настоящая замша. Затем была легендарная сумка Neverfull от LV из кожи Empreinte. Купила её в магазине LV в торговом центре Galleria Mall в Далласе. Заплатила 2700 долларов, в 6 раз дороже, чем Coach Theo. Это странно, потому что в автомобилях, если вы платите в шесть раз больше, вы явно видите и чувствуете разницу в цене. Представьте себе Chevrolet Malibu за 25 000 долларов против Mercedes S550 за 130 тысяч. Это совершенно разные вещи, и вы легко можете это почувствовать. В кожаных изделиях разница абсолютно незаметна, по крайней мере, снаружи. Дизайн LV — культовый. Вот почему она немного дороже, чем у Coach. Конструкция обеих сумок хорошо продумана с точки зрения формы и функциональности. Честно говоря, мне кожа Coach, обработанная методом дубления, нравится гораздо больше, чем Empreinte на сумке Neverfull. Тем не менее, обе сумки — отличный выбор кожи. Фурнитура на Neverfull минимальна, но уровень качества у обеих сумок очень сопоставим. У Neverfull было несколько торчащих ниток и незначительные дефекты изготовления. С другой стороны, сумка Coach Theo безупречна. Я не нашел никаких недостатков. Единственным разочарованием при покупке Coach стало утверждение продавца о том, что внутренняя подкладка сделана из замши, а на самом деле это была ткань. Качество этих двух сумок примерно одинаковое. Но шестикратную разницу в цене игнорировать нельзя. Теперь давайте посмотрим на сравнение, которое изменило для меня все. Coach Pillow Tabby против LV Go-14 Pico. Схожий размер, похожий стиль, похожая кожа. Pillow Tabby. Я купила эту сумку в магазине Coach в Далласе за 395 долларов. Отличное обслуживание, на этот раз точная информация. LV Go-14 Pico. Эту я купила в флагманском магазине Louis Vuitton в Париже за 4450 долларов. Теперь разница в цене в 11 раз больше. Давайте сначала посмотрим на качество. Go-14 отличается исключительным дизайном. Мелкие детали, которые в совокупности создают это великолепное исполнение. Pillow Tabby тоже хороша, но ей не хватает этих нюансов. Здесь LV выигрывает. Сложный дизайн Go-14 требует большего инженерного мастерства, и они его продемонстрировали. LV использовала здесь первоклассную овечью кожу. Кожа Coach тоже хороша, но не на элитном уровне. Фурнитура LV здесь действительно блестит. Фурнитура Coach тоже ценная, но средняя и более чем соответствует цене. Суть в качестве изготовления. Вот что действительно важно. Обе сумки сравниваются напрямую. Одна изготовлена во Вьетнаме, другая в Италии, но уровень мастерства в обеих исключителен. Это победа для LV, но разница настолько незначительна. От очень хорошей сумки к великолепной, а не от хорошей к в 11 раз лучше. В этот момент мое замешательство усилилось. Что я упускал? Некоторые из вас могут подумать: «Но Таннер, ты упускаешь из виду коммерческие расходы, команды дизайнеров, операционную деятельность и магазины». Дело в том, что и у LV, и у Coach есть эти расходы. Так откуда же берется оправдание такой огромной разницы в цене? Подсказка пришла ко мне, когда я вошел в этот флагманский магазин, чтобы купить Go-14. Это был не просто магазин. Это был храм современной роскоши. Сочетание музея и художественной выставки, демонстрирующее историю бренда. Освещение, пространство, впечатления. Было ощущение, будто я попал в нечто возвышенное, эксклюзивное, недоступное. Это существенно отличается от магазинов Coach. Даже от их флагманского магазина в Нью-Йорке. Я купил там сумку Rogue. Исключительный продукт, потрясающие люди, но там не было такого же уровня зрелищности. Вот тогда-то и всё встало на свои места. При создании продукта из кожи нужно выполнить две задачи. Во-первых, создать само мастерство обработки кожи. Моя пятиступенчатая модель основана именно на этом: дизайн, структура, кожа, фурнитура, мастерство. Это важно, но это только половина работы. Во-вторых, создать восприятие, невидимый аспект продукта. Почти как его тень. Вот как я это понимаю сейчас. Такие бренды, как Coach, сосредотачиваются на том, чтобы сделать продукт максимально качественным, и позволяют продукту нести свою собственную тень, естественное отражение его физических качеств. История создаётся через тактильные ощущения, использование, долгосрочное удовлетворение, сарафанное радио и разумный объём маркетинга, чтобы донести качество и ценности бренда. Такие бренды, как LV, позиционирующие себя как современные люксовые марки, работают иначе. Они создают ауру вокруг своих продуктов и логотипов. Они продают не просто сумку. Они продают символ статуса. Эта иллюзия подкрепляется заметными логотипами и высокими ценами, которые сигнализируют об исключительной ценности, даже если это не сразу видно на самом продукте. Высокие цены позволяют брендам тратить огромные средства на рекламные кампании со знаменитостями, показы мод и постоянно меняющиеся, похожие на храмы, витрины магазинов. Все это призвано поддерживать высокий уровень имиджа. Эта модель в значительной степени опирается на человеческое стремление к статусу и признанию. Она дорога и сопряжена с рисками, потому что основным продуктом является восприятие, которое должно быть принято обществом, иначе все это – обман и иллюзия. Итак, перед нами два крупных бренда с очень похожим качеством продукции, глобальным присутствием и операционными затратами, но с резко отличающимися ценами. Разрыв можно объяснить только восприятием. Искусственно созданная иллюзия, которую один бренд решил построить, а другой – нет. Каждый продукт состоит из двух частей: что это такое и что это значит. Coach создает продукты. Louis Vuitton производит желания, и эти желания стоят дополнительных денег. И вот здесь начинается самое интересное. Эти бренды не просто случайно попали в игру с восприятием. Они используют специально разработанные психологические приемы, чтобы заставить вас платить больше. В своем последнем видео, «Путешествие с LV», я поделился четырьмя психологическими приемами, которые вы можете использовать против этих гениальных манипуляций люксовых брендов. Несколько простых мысленных экспериментов, которые помогут вам взглянуть на вещи реально и убедиться, что вы делаете осознанный выбор, а не решение, принятое под влиянием манипуляций. Если вы думаете: «Подождите, мне нужно научиться этому, чтобы защитить себя», то это видео изменит ваше отношение к каждой покупке предметов роскоши в будущем. Я оставлю ссылку на это видео в конце и в описании. Но сначала посмотрите это видео, потому что есть еще одна вещь, которую вам нужно знать. В следующий раз, когда вы будете выбирать между сумкой Coach и Louis Vuitton или любой другой подобной сумкой, не спрашивайте себя, что лучше по качеству. Спросите себя: я покупаю продукт или символ? И если вы покупаете символ, стоит ли этот символ для вас в 6 или 11 раз дороже? Потому что вот правда, которую никто в мире роскоши не хочет, чтобы вы поняли. Как только вы увидите разницу между мастерством и восприятием, Вы становитесь неуязвимы для манипуляций. И именно поэтому я продолжаю разрезать эти сумки, чтобы показать вам это. Продукт есть продукт. История, связанная с ним, — это ваш выбор . Все выборы справедливы, если они осознанны и продуманы. Если это исследование оказалось для вас полезным, не забудьте подписаться, поскольку я продолжаю рассказывать вам о реальном качестве и иллюзии в современной роскоши. А если вы хотите узнать о моих четырех психологических приемах защиты от этих манипуляций в мире роскоши, посмотрите следующее видео LV Journey. Потому что знание правил игры — это половина дела. Как всегда, до новых встреч, оставайтесь в кожаной одежде!

- The Hidden Psychology That Drives Unnecessary Purchases: Kind: captions Language: en You finally pull the trigger on that high-end premium leather sofa. You've been eyeing it for months. And as you swipe your card for $2,000, you aren't just buying a place to sit. You're buying a new version of yourself. A version that is settled, sophisticated, and finally arrived. You imagine guests walking in and thinking, "Wow, they've really made it." The sofa arrives and it smells like success. It's the centerpiece of your life. But as soon as you sit down, you look across at your old scratched up particle board coffee table. Suddenly, that table, the one that served you perfectly for 3 years, looks like literal junk. It's no longer a table. It's a stain on your new identity. It whispers that you're still just an amateur pretending to be a professional. The truth is, your luxury purchase didn't come alone. It brought a silent judgment that you aren't yet enough. This clashing vibe starts to keep you up at night. You can't have a masterpiece sitting next to a dumpster find. So, you go back online and drop $500 on a modern marble top table just to match the aesthetic. But wait, now the rug underneath looks faded and cheap. You can't have a $500 table on a $40 rug, right? That's another $300 for designer wool. Then you realize the curtains are the wrong shade of blue. The wall paint looks dull in this new light. And suddenly, you're hiring a painter and buying $200 worth of decorative pillows. Within a week, that $2,000 investment in yourself has quietly bullied you into a $4,000 000 renovation you never planned for. And you didn't even see the blackmail coming. This isn't just you being impulsive. This is a psychological chain reaction that has drained more bank accounts than the stock market ever could. It explains why a simple promotion often leads to a hollow bank account and why buying a dream car can turn into a financial nightmare. My name is Jim and on this channel we explore the invisible scripts that keep us broke. Most people think they buy things because they need them. Today I'm going to prove to you that you buy things because your other things are demanding them. You think you're the master of your house, but you're actually just a servant to your own furniture. To understand this spiral, we have to look back at 1913 at a man named Arthur Pop moment. Arthur and his wife moved to a wealthy community in New York. They were doing well, but they weren't New York elite well. He began to suffer from what I call social friction. Every time he looked over the fence, his neighbors had a newer carriage, a bigger house, and more elegant parties. Arthur felt a psychological itch. It wasn't that his life was bad. It was that his life didn't match his surroundings. He felt he had to look the part to be taken seriously. He started spending every cent he earned to bridge the gap. New furniture to match the neighborhood prestige, a better car to match the driveway next door, finer clothes so he wouldn't look like a fraud at the local club. Arthur wasn't buying things to use them. He was buying them to purchase the approval of people who didn't even like him. Eventually, the weight of his pride, his social ego, crushed him. He went completely broke trying to maintain a lifestyle he couldn't afford. Terrified of being the only one left behind. He turned his failure into a famous comic strip called Keeping Up with the Joneses. Here's the kicker. In the comic, the Joneses never actually appear. They are a ghost, a shadow. And that is the terrifying reality of the matching trap. We aren't just trying to match our things. We are trying to match a social standard that doesn't even exist. Most of us think we are immune to this, but modern marketing has weaponized your fear of inongruity. It's called identity upgrading. Now, you might be thinking, "Okay, Jim, I don't live in some fancy New York zip code and I don't have wealthy neighbors to compete with. What does this have to do with me?" The truth is it relates to you every single day. It just hides under modern names. Leveling up my lifestyle. Investing in myself or the most common one status. Be honest. Have you ever found yourself here? You get a big bonus or a promotion to celebrate. You buy the latest MacBook Pro. You tell yourself, "It's a tool. It makes me more productive." But the moment you set that space gray masterpiece on your scratched IKEA desk, a seed of discomfort grows. Suddenly, the desk you've used happily for 5 years looks cheap. You feel like an amateur, so you drop $800 on a premium standing desk to match the energy of the laptop. But then you realize your old swivel chair looks pathetic. Sitting in that beatup chair at this high-end desk feels like an insult to your lower back. So, you swipe for a $1,500 Herman Miller Aeron. It doesn't stop there. The tangled mess of cables in the old lamp are ruining the professional vibe. You buy a cable kit, a $200 screen bar, and minimalist speakers. In less than a month, a single laptop has quietly bullied you into a $5,000 office renovation. You didn't do it because the old desk was broken. You did it because you were afraid that if your things didn't match, you weren't actually the success you claimed to be. This is the Dero effect. It turns a luxury purchase into your new baseline. It makes us buy things we don't need with money we don't have to impress a version of ourselves that doesn't exist. We think we own our things, but that new gadget is actually the boss ordering you to buy 10 other things to serve it. So, how do we break a cycle wired into our dopamine receptors? We can't just live in a cave, but we can build a psychological circuit breaker. Strategy one, the domino audit. Before any anchor purchase, a car, a handbag, a camera, map out the dominoes. Ask, "What is the total cost of the system, not just the sticker price?" If that $1,000 500 camera demands a $400 lens and a $2,000 laptop upgrade, the cost is $4,000. If you can't afford the $4,000 ecosystem, don't buy the entry fee. Stop looking at items in isolation and start seeing them as an entry fee to an expensive club you might not be ready for. Strategy two, the onein, oneout inventory cap. The Ditto effect thrives on accumulation. We add the new but keep the old, creating a clash we feel forced to fix. You must cap your inventory. If you buy new sneakers, an old pair must leave. If you upgrade the coffee machine, the old one goes. This forces you to value what you have and keeps you in the mindset of a curator, not a consumer. Strategy three, embrace the status anchor. This is the nuclear option. Deliberately keep one visible part of your life low status. Drive a reliable dented 10-year-old car while earning a six-f figure salary. Wear a $20 Casio with a tailor made suit. When you intentionally keep one anchor item humble, you break the spell of perfection. If your car is basic, you feel zero pressure to stay at five-star hotels just to match your vehicle. You give yourself the freedom to be mismatched. A lowmaintenance identity is the most profitable asset you can own. It is the ultimate financial cheat code. It allows you to stack real wealth while everyone else is exhausting their bank accounts trying to curate a living museum for people. they don't even like. Consumerism is engineered to make you feel perpetually incomplete. That you are always just one purchase away from being enough. But Arthur Mullman and the man with the bathrobe prove the truth. That one purchase doesn't finish the puzzle. It scramles it. It resets the board and forces you to start the expensive game of matching all over again. Don't let a piece of leather or a titanium frame dictate your worth. Don't be a slave to your possessions. Be the master of your life. - Steal the Psychology of Luxury: 5-Star Hotel Tricks for a More Elegant Home: Kind: captions Language: en You're spending thousands on furniture, but your space still feels average. Meanwhile, some people walk into a modest apartment and instantly think, "Wow, this feels expensive." The difference, they know what five-star establishments know. And today, I'm revealing everything. Stick around because by the end of this video, you'll understand exactly why luxury spaces feel the way they do and you'll have the tools to recreate that magic in your own home. If you're not subscribed to Senior's Cleaning Hacks yet, hit that subscribe button now because we're about to share industry secrets that will completely transform how you view your living space. And please share this with someone who needs to hear it. The bedroom revolution. creating a sleep experience worth thousands. Let me start by addressing the biggest question everyone asks. Why does sleeping in a hotel feel like floating on a cloud, but your own bed feels basic? It's not the mattress. This is the shocking truth that most people get wrong. Bed manufacturers want you to spend thousands on new mattresses, but professional luxury designers know better. The real magic happens in the layers. And today, Marcus from Senior's Cleaning Hacks is breaking down exactly how. Trick number one, master the triple sheet method. Hotels don't use one fitted sheet and call it done. They layer strategically. The bottom layer is your fitted sheet pulled tight and tucked perfectly. Then comes the first flat sheet, crisp and smooth. Next is your duvet encased in a beautiful cover. Finally, another flat sheet sits on top of everything. folded back at the perfect angle for visual appeal and function. Why does this work so well? Each layer creates air flow. None of them trap heat against your body. As you shift during the night, you're sliding across multiple surfaces, and each one feels premium because it's loose enough to breathe. This is why hotels never oversted their beds with blankets. Instead, they perfect this layering system. The texture combination matters, too. Some sheets are linen, some are cotton blends, and some are satine finish. When you mix these textures across your layers, your skin experiences different sensations throughout the night, and your brain registers that as luxury. One material throughout feels monotonous. Variety feels intentional and high-end. Trick number two, the pillow combination strategy. Your pillows might be the biggest reason your bed doesn't feel expensive. Think about what you have right now. If they're flat, if they've lost their shape, if they don't support your head properly, your entire sleep experience suffers. Luxury hotels use a combination approach. They place down pillows alongside down alternative pillows on each bed. Here's why this matters. Down pillows conform to your head beautifully but can go flat over time. Down alternative pillows provide structure and last longer. By combining them, you get the soft, conforming feel of down with the lasting support of synthetic materials. Most people buy one type of pillow. The result is unbalanced comfort. You need variety. Four pillows on a queen bed isn't excessive in the luxury world. It's standard. Two are fluffy. Two are slightly firmer. You arrange them so that no matter how you sleep, you're resting your head on the ideal combination. Don't forget about pillowcase quality either. The feel of what touches your face matters immensely. Higher thread count pillow cases create a smoother glide for your skin. Your face slides across them effortlessly. Lower quality cases create friction. That friction, even though it's subtle, your brain registers as uncomfortable. Trick number three, the duvet doubling technique. This is the secret that transforms everything about your bed's visual appearance and comfort level. Take one duvet and place it inside a second duvet cover. You'll have two duvet sitting on top of each other, both covered. The effect is dramatic. Your bed looks impossibly plush. When you lie down, you're not just supported by one layer of filling. You're surrounded by double the insulation and comfort. Hotels use this technique constantly because it's affordable yet creates the illusion of luxury. The weight feels different, too. A single duvet might feel thin or wimpy when you first sit on the bed. Double it up and suddenly you feel enveloped. Your body temperature regulation improves because you have more insulation options. You can pull one duvet up or adjust both independently. This technique costs nothing if you already have two duvet. If you don't, buying a second affordable duvet is far cheaper than upgrading your mattress. Yet, the comfort improvement rivals a new mattress purchase. Lighting, where the real magic happens. Here's something most people never consider. Luxury has more to do with light than with actual furniture. Walk through your home right now and notice something. Are you relying on one or two overhead lights to illuminate your entire room? That's why it doesn't feel premium. Professional lighting designers use three distinct types of light working together. And this is trick number four. Trick number four, ambient lighting foundation. Ambient light is your room's baseline illumination. It's not bright, it's not focused. It's the soft, warm glow that makes the space feel welcoming. Hotels achieve this using wall sconces, floor lamps positioned in corners, and hidden LED strip lighting tucked behind furniture or crown molding. The color temperature matters enormously. Warm white light around 2700 Kelvin creates a relaxing atmosphere. Cool white light around 4,000 Kelvin feels clinical and institutional. That's why hospitals and offices use cool light, but homes should feel warm. Strategic placement is crucial. Position lamps where they create gentle shadows, not where they shine directly into your eyes. When light bounces off walls and ceilings before reaching you, it feels diffused and luxurious. Direct light feels harsh. Trick number five, task lighting for functionality. Task lighting serves a purpose. It's the lamp on your nightstand, the light above your sink, the fixture over your reading chair. It provides enough illumination to do something specific without lighting the entire room to hospital brightness levels. A bedside lamp with a warm bulb allows you to read comfortably without activating your room's main lights. This is essential luxury design. You can have multiple lighting modes in one space. Evening reading mode, winding down mode, getting ready mode. Each has its own lighting setup. Consider lampshades carefully. A lampshade diffuses light and prevents direct glare. A naked bulb in a fixture feels cheap. The same bulb behind quality linen or fabric becomes elegant. It's the same light, but it's filtered and refined. Trick number six, accent lighting for drama. Accent lights are subtle but noticeable. They highlight specific areas or objects. Under cabinet lighting in kitchens makes the workspace feel premium and functional. A small light behind artwork makes pieces stand out. Candles in the bathroom create atmosphere. These lights aren't bright. They're not meant to illuminate the entire space. They're meant to draw attention to carefully chosen elements, a vase, a piece of art, an architectural feature. When you guide people's eyes to beautiful elements through strategic lighting, they perceive the entire space [music] as more intentional and expensive. The biggest lighting mistake people make is using only overhead lights with bright cold bulbs. No luxury space is lit this way. Every space in a high-end environment uses multiple light sources, warm tones, and deliberate dimming. Install dimmer switches on your ambient lights. This gives you complete control over the mood. Bright enough to clean, dim enough to relax. This is not an expensive upgrade, but it transforms everything. The invisible luxury. How scent transforms your space. Most people focus on what they see and touch in their homes. Scent is overlooked, yet it's one of the most powerful luxury indicators. When you step into a five-star hotel, the air itself feels expensive. Trick number seven, essential oil diffusion system. Replace standard air fresheners with essential oil diffusers. Air fresheners are chemical heavy and feel artificial. Quality essential oils when properly diffused create a scent that feels natural and intentional. A combination like bergamont and vanilla creates a spa-like atmosphere. Lavender and eucalyptus promotes relaxation. Lemon and orange energizes. The key is choosing scent profiles that align with how you want the space to feel. Ultrasonic diffusers are affordable and effective. They break essential oils into micro particles that disperse throughout your space naturally. The scent isn't overwhelming. It's subtle and lingers without being aggressive. Change your scent seasonally. Citrus notes for spring and summer, warm spice and vanilla for fall and winter. This creates the feeling that your home is being professionally maintained and updated, which is how luxury spaces operate. Trick number eight, scented linen treatment. Take your bedding and pillows to the next level by lightly misting them with scented spray. Use quality linen spray, not cheap perfume. Lavender scented linen spray is the luxury standard because lavender is associated with sleep, relaxation, and high-end spa environments. The mist should be light. You're not drowning your linens in scent. You're creating a subtle aromatic experience. When your head touches the pillow, that gentle scent helps your brain transition into relaxation mode. It's psychological, yes, but psychology is powerful. Do this right before bed, not hours in advance. The scent should be fresh and present as you prepare to sleep. Trick number nine, strategic candle placement. Real candles in specific locations create ambiance and scent without overwhelming a room. But here's the luxury trick that most people miss. Place candles in spaces where guests won't see them. Your bedroom closet, bathroom drawers, under sinks. Candles in these hidden locations scent your fabrics, your clothes, your entire environment with luxury fragrance. When you wear a shirt or open a drawer, that elevated scent emerges. It makes everything feel pampered and intentional. Rotate candle scents throughout your home. Different rooms can have different profiles. Your bedroom stays calm and relaxing. Your living room feels sophisticated and inviting. Your kitchen smells fresh and clean. This consistency across multiple scent environments creates a cohesive luxury experience. Decluttering is the foundation of elegance. Luxury and clutter cannot coexist. This is perhaps the most important concept in the entire video. A $50,000 sofa in a cluttered room looks wrong. A $500 sofa in a well organized, minimal space looks expensive. Trick number 10, the intentional design principle. Luxury is never about having more. It's about curating better. Every single item in a high-end space serves a purpose or brings joy. Nothing is randomly placed. Nothing competes for attention. Start by looking at your rooms honestly. What items don't contribute to how the space makes you feel? That decorative item you've never looked at twice. The stack of magazines from 6 months ago. The furniture piece you keep bumping into. These things are working against you. Remove them. Not because minimalism is trendy, but because clutter literally makes spaces feel cheap. Your brain registers mess as disorganization and low value. Emptiness and intentionality register as high value. Trick number 11. Furniture arrangement for flow. Oversized furniture shoved against every wall doesn't create luxury. Strategic placement does. Leave some breathing room. Allow people to move through the space naturally. Create conversation areas where furniture faces each other, not all facing one wall like a cinema. A room with space to move through and breathe feels bigger, more organized, and more expensive. This doesn't mean empty rooms. It means purposeful arrangement. The finishing touches, premium details that cost nothing. You've sorted lighting, scent, and clutter. Now comes the final layer that separates ordinary homes from luxury ones. Trick number 12, luxury towel strategy. Walk into any high-end bathroom and the towels immediately communicate quality. They're large, they're thick, they're luxurious, white or neutral tones. Small, thin towels feel budgetoriented. Large, plush towels feel expensive. Replace your current towels with oversized, highquality versions. The difference in daily experience is remarkable. These towels cost moderately more, but transform your entire bathroom aesthetic and function. Fold them neatly. Stack them on shelves. Small touches like this create visual organization that reads as luxury. Trick number 13, trays and layering. Hotels organize bathroom items and bedroom surfaces using decorative trays. A tray on your nightstand corrals perfume bottles, a lamp, a book. A tray on your bathroom counter organizes your skinare items. A tray on your coffee table creates a curated display of books and a candle. Trays prevent random scattering of items. They create visual organization and hierarchy. Instead of 10 things spread across a surface, you have a contained, thoughtful arrangement. This technique transforms clutter into intentional styling. The exact same items scattered look chaotic. The same items on a tray look curated. Trick number 14. Mirrors and reflective surfaces. Strategic mirror placement makes small spaces feel larger and brighter. A mirror opposite a window reflects natural light throughout the room. Mirrors across from artwork make the space feel deeper. Reflective surfaces create visual expansion. This is why hotel rooms, even small ones, feel spacious. Mirrors are positioned to enhance light and create depth. Trick number 15, personal artwork. Generic mass-roduced artwork from big box stores reads as temporary and inexpensive. Framed personal photographs or black and white prints feel intentional and sophisticated. Select photos that matter to you. Frame them uniformly in black or natural wood frames. Arrange them in a gallery wall or small grouping. This costs far less than buying art, yet creates the impression of a carefully curated personal space. The key is consistency in framing. Mixed frames and sizes look chaotic. Uniform frames and thoughtful spacing look designed and Now that you understand the 15 core tricks, let's talk about implementation. You don't need to do everything at once. Luxury design is about layers and progression. Start with lighting. This single upgrade impacts every single space and costs moderately. Swap harsh overhead lights for warm bulbs and add dimmer switches. Introduce floor lamps and accent lighting. Next, tackle your bedding. Triple sheeting and pillow combination plus duvet doubling happen this week. Your sleep experience transforms immediately. Then focus on decluttering one room at a time. Remove everything that doesn't serve a purpose. Arrange remaining items with intentionality. Add scent to your environment using diffusers and linen spray. This invisible luxury component takes days to implement but compounds the luxury feeling considerably. Finally, add the finishing touches. Upgrade your towels. Introduce trays for organization. Hang personal artwork. Position mirrors strategically. Creating a luxury home experience isn't about wealth. It's about understanding the principles that high-end spaces follow and implementing them strategically. - Comparing the BIG three mattress types: Kind: captions Language: en hi everyone I'm David Rubin director of product testing at the sleepdoctor.com and I'm Dr Michael Bruce the sleep doctor the best type of mattress for you depends on several factors like your body weight and favorite sleeping position while there are many mattress designs on the market today each type targets the needs of different sleepers let's check out the different mattress types first let's look at memory foam mattresses memory foam mattresses offer exceptional pressure relief in motion isolation and they have a tendency to have a lower price point than latex or hybrid beds this mattress type typically suits side sleepers or anyone who needs substantial pressure relief also foam Contours closely to the body providing a hugging Sensation that cushions joints it also prevents motion from transferring across the mattress surface which can be an added benefit for couples or co-sleepers and if your Co-Sleeper is of the furry variety remember try keeping your animal towards the end of the bed as they do have a tendency to migrate quite a bit throughout the night one thing to also keep in mind foam retain remains heat and can sleep hotter than other types of mattresses so keep that in mind if you're a hot sleeper next we have latex beds natural latex comes from the sap of rubber trees and gives mattresses a resilient and very responsive feeling the material lacks the extreme contouring of memory foam which makes it a worthwhile pick for sleepers who need pressure relief but don't like to sink too deeply into the mattress and since latex regulates temperature well sleepers are unlikely to overheat on a Model that includes it this style generally costs more to manufacture the memory foam mattresses resulting in higher end price points and while latex mattresses are durable they can also be heavy so make sure you have someone with you when you're moving in or setting up the bed and finally we have hybrid and inner spring mattresses both hybrid and inner spring mattresses have coil support cores and some manufacturers use these terms interchangeably however they have two distinct designs on top of the coil core hybrids often have comfort systems that incorporate foam fiber wool or latex to enhance performance and Target different needs traditional coil or inner spring mattresses May provide extra cushioning by adding micro coils pillow tops or Euro tops and while the exact specifications between coil gauges and styles may vary by manufacturer all hybrids and inner Springs share some similar characteristics also the space created by the coil base allows air to circulate within the mattress and reduces heat retention making these a great option for hot sleepers and the coils also provide a responsive surface that prevents sleepers from sinking too deeply into the mattress hybrid mattresses typically use pocketed coils that are individually wrapped and move independently of one another resulting in less motion transfer than a traditional inner spring mattress additionally inner spring mattresses often provide robust support though they can lack cushioning while this may be advantageous for some back and stomach sleepers side sleepers may want to consider if an inner spring model is plush enough to prevent pressure points before committing to a purchase when choosing a mattress you need to consider two key factors the first is your body weight and the second is your sleep position your body weight dictates How firm of a bed will give you the right balance of support and pressure relief in general the more you weigh the more firm your mattress should be while a soft mattress may be perfect for someone under about 130 pounds someone over 230 pounds May struggle to get the support they're looking for yeah and additionally each mattress type and firmness have different benefits depending on your sleep position side sleepers require the most cushioning of all sleeper types when you sleep on your side much of your body weight rests in your hips and shoulders these areas can develop uncomfortable pressure points if a mattress doesn't Contour to the sleeper the best mattresses for side sleeper reduces pressure points and prevents your midsection from sinking into the mattress and your spine from falling out of alignment regardless of weight side sleepers should look for a mattress that offers plenty of contouring to prevent sore hips and shoulders latex foam and certain hybrid models are well suited to the task on the other hand back sleepers benefit from mattresses that combine support and pressure relief while they tend not to feel the same acute pressure points as side sleepers back sleepers can develop soreness or back pain if they sleep on a mattress that lacks the proper cushioning for back sleepers the best mattress features a mid-range firmness this can vary according to your weight hybrid and memory foam mattresses are a good fit for most back sleepers just be sure it's firm enough to keep your body in proper alignment and stomach sleepers need the firmest mattress of all a mattress that's too soft can cause the spine to fall out of alignment resulting in pain or discomfort the best mattress for stomach sleepers offers slight contouring to soften their sleep surface but a mattress with too much cushioning can cause stomach sleepers to feel as if they're sinking into the mattress we recommend a firmer hybrid and latex mattress for these sleepers and finally finding the right model for combination sleepers can be tricky as they need to consider multiple sleeping positions sleepers within this group should look for a model with the firmness level that's appropriate for each sleeping position within their weight range for example if you're someone who's on the Lighter Side think 130 pounds or less look for a bed that is about a four to six on the firmness scale if you're someone in the 130 to 230 pound range consider a bed around seven to eight on the firmness scale and if you're over 230 pounds look for something in the 8 to 10 range and for more information on these firmness levels check out our resources on the sleep doctor dot com that's it for this one everyone this is David Rubin and I'm Dr Michael Bruce the sleep doctor wishing you sweet dreams

- Buying your next mattress: What to look for: Kind: captions Language: en Hey, how's it going? This is Owen with cnet.com and in today's video, we're going to try and answer the question for you, how should you go about shopping for your next mattress. [Music] Now, having the right mattress for you and your body type and your sleeping preferences is super important considering we spend about a third of our lives sleeping. And considering the amount of places you can buy a mattress, how many kinds of mattresses you can find, it is a pretty daunting decision. and you want to make sure you get it right because a mattress can be a pretty sizable investment. And we're going to have a bunch of questions for you to answer to help guide that decision-making process. Now, we're going to have a lot of information linked for you in the description to help you out with this process, including some best list reviews of popular mattresses. All right, let's just dive right into this video. Starting with question number one, which is probably the most obvious one, but it's nonetheless important, is do you actually need a new mattress? The average lifespan for a mattress is seven to 10 years depending on the quality of the bed. And if you start to notice body impressions or it's sagging in certain places or it's making a lot of noise when you're tossing and turning, odds are your mattress is nearing the end of its lifespan and you need a new one. Or it could just be that your mattress isn't that old, but you just find it to be extremely uncomfortable or you're waking up with aches and pains a lot. then that's a good sign that you might need a new mattress that better suits your body type and sleeping preferences. If the answer to this question is yes, you do need a new mattress, you should consider your current mattress and figure out what you like and don't like about it. Do you like the feel of the bed? Is it a memory foam bed? Do you like how soft or firm it is? Do you like the responsiveness? Or do you want something that has a little more sink in? Consider all of these factors of your current mattress before you want to shop for a new one. And then the next thing you need to consider is the actual size of the mattress you want to get. Are you going to upgrade to a king size, stick with the size you currently have, or maybe you're moving to a smaller space? That's something to consider. For me personally, my bedroom setup really does not accommodate a king size, so we're stuck with a queen. But that's just something you need to keep in mind for your next mattress purchase. And also, depending on which brand you end up going with, Brooklyn Bedding specifically comes to mind. may offer some more unique sizes than the standard twin, full, queen, and king. So, that's something to keep in mind as well. Once you have all this stuff figured out, that's when you start getting into more specific questions. And in my opinion, as someone who's tested out hundreds of mattresses, the most important thing you need to figure out is how soft or firm you want your next mattress to be. Now, this is a particularly difficult thing to nail down because in general, firmness is pretty subjective. If you're heavier like me, you will generally feel beds to be a bit softer than someone who's much lighter than me. We've tested out hundreds of beds over here, and we try our best to rate beds for the average sized individual. Just keep that in mind as you're reading about firmness. Now, deciding on firmness for your next mattress is really going to depend on your preferred sleeping position. There are three main sleeping positions: back, stomach, and side. And many people are a combination of more than one of these. Now, in general, if you're a back or stomach sleeper, you're probably going to prefer a firmer mattress with a little less give. This is because if you're sleeping on your back and the mattress sinks in too much, you could develop back pain over time because your spine isn't in a straight line while you're sleeping. And then, conversely, people who spend the majority of the night on their side tend to prefer a softer mattress that helps cradle their pressure points like their hips and their shoulders. But if you are a combination sleeper who spends a good portion of the night in any one of those three positions, you might want something closer to a medium because it can accommodate all three fairly well. I was actually helping my aunt shop for a new mattress and she is a primary side sleeper, but her old mattress was pretty firm. I suggested something softer and she's sleeping much better now. Once you have an idea of how soft or firm you want your next mattress to be, you can start thinking about your preference on feel and materials. Now, we tend to categorize mattress feels in a couple different ways. We have memory foam feels on one end and then more responsive feels on the other end. Memory foam beds generally have a pretty distinctive feel. You lay down on one of these things and then it's going to feel fairly firm at first and then that memory foam is going to soften up and you're going to sink into it and it's going to remember your body's shape. Beds like Tempropedic, Nectar, Leila, Amerasleep all feature some kind of a memory foam feel and this is what a lot of sleepers out there are looking for. And then on the other end of the spectrum you have beds that are much more responsive that you don't really sink into. These are beds like Avocado, Sautva, even some Helix mattresses. Those are going to have a more responsive feel that are very dissimilar to memory foam. And then you have beds that fall somewhere in the middle that have more of a neutral feel. This means that don't really feel very distinctive and they're just trying to be generally comfortable for most sleepers. These are going to be beds like Casper and Lisa and Ghost Bed. Just pretty comfortable for most people, even if it's not exactly what you're looking for. Figuring out your particular preference on feel and materials can be pretty difficult if you don't have tons of beds to test out. But this might be a nice opportunity to go to your local mattress store and just try out a bunch of beds and see what you like. And this is a perfect opportunity to ask the question, where should you get your next mattress? Obviously, there are lots of brick-and-mortar stores you can buy from, and there are dozens and dozens of online brands that can ship a mattress directly to your door. Now, the online method has become more and more popular over the years, and one of the big reasons for that is their included trial period. You get at least 100 nights, if not longer, with pretty much every online brand to sleep on the mattress at home to decide if you actually like it or not. And then the return process is usually really quick and easy. Normally, you just call the company up and they'll send out a local team to pick up the mattress for you and donate it to a local charity and then you get all your money back. It might vary a bit from brand to brand, but that is how the majority of them work. Now, most brick-and-mortar stores will also offer a very similar trial period, but there is essentially very little difference to going to a store and having a bed delivered and getting one sent to you online. When you buy online, you shouldn't have to pay anything extra for shipping, and they should all come with a standard 10-year warranty, if not longer. So, you can kind of zero in on a mattress you're interested in, have it shipped to your door, and then if you end up liking it, that's fantastic. But if you don't, you can initiate the return process and try out another one risk-free. We have a few more questions to cover. One big one is, are you getting this mattress for yourself or are you going to be sharing it with a partner? There are two pretty big factors to consider when you are buying a mattress that's meant to be shared. one of which is edge support and the other is motion isolation. Now, edge support refers to how well the edges of the mattress hold up under pressure. This is pretty important if you're sharing a smaller size mattress and you need to utilize the entire surface area of the bed. Now, edge support is something we test in every single mattress we review, so be sure to check out that section if it's important to you. And most mattress brands do a pretty good job of edge support. Most of the time, they have extra reinforced coils along the edges for this reason, but it is something to keep in mind. Now, motion isolation refers to how well the mattress absorbs cross mattress motion. I'm sure you've seen the wine tests they do in those Tempropedic commercials from way back. And again, this is something that most beds do quite well, but an all foam memory foam bed is going to perform a bit better than a latex hybrid. Again, it is something that is included in all of our written reviews. And then the last question you need to ask yourself is, what is your budget for a new mattress? I recommend setting a range of a high-end and a low-end and then finding all the beds that fit that range and shopping around. You should be able to find a nice variety of beds within most price ranges from sub500 to over 1500. Now, just because a mattress is really expensive, it doesn't mean it's the best. I would definitely shop around, do your research, do as much comparison shopping as possible to find the one that you think is the best value and will work best for your particular sleeping preferences. And again, like I mentioned, there is a trial period for most of these beds. So, if you end up choosing wrong, it should be pretty easy to try out another bed on your list. And when it comes to saving money on a brand new mattress, there are many discounts running year round on a lot of these online mattress brands. - 5 Things NOT to do when buying a new mattress: Kind: captions Language: en Now, folks, five quick points on what not to do when buying a mattress. Very first thing, if you are buying just a mattress on its own, make sure you have the base that suits the mattress. Now, what I mean by that, if you're buying a mattress that has full memory foam, make sure the base hasn't got slats. The reason being is memory foam works on both sides. So, if you put a mattress directly onto a slat, it's going to sink into it. So, what you would need is then a flat divan base. Like that, for a full memory foam mattress. Yeah. Point number two. When you're buying a mattress in a shop, don't be embarrassed. It's the worst thing that can you can do. Sit on a mattress. But, don't sit on a mattress as some people do. As I said before, you sit like this, if that mattress has pocket springs in it, you will sink. You must lie across it. The reason you lie across it is because it has all the pocket springs, your weight is now dispersed. Make sure, that's a very important fact. Now, folks, point number three. If you're buying a mattress, don't buy a mattress because somebody else likes it. Buy the mattress that's good for you. So, if you have a memory foam mattress, memory foam heats up. So, this is a very important fact that you have. When you lie on a mattress, the memory foam curls around your body, and it heats up to your body temperature. So, if you're a warm sleeper at nighttime, my advice, our advice here, is to stay clear of a memory foam mattress. Doesn't mean you can't have a soft, comfortable mattress. You can have a latex mattress. You can have a gel tex mattress. Does exactly what the memory foam does, but doesn't heat up. So, if you're a warm sleeper, keep that in mind. Point number four. Don't buy a mattress because of the price. What I mean is, the dearest mattress you can buy doesn't necessarily means the best mattress for you. It could be the worst thing you'll ever buy. Cheapest mattress that you could find doesn't necessarily mean it's cheap if it suits you properly. Lie on them, check them all out. You never know which one is good for you. And finally, point number five, the most important one, is don't do a quick buy. Take your time. Remember, a third of your life is spent lying in that mattress. So, make sure you spend your time looking at it properly. You don't tire kick a car when you're when you're trying to buy it. Don't do it to a mattress. You spend more time in that than you do in anything else. Talk to you soon.

So how do you even know if you need a new mattress? Well, there are a few quick ways to answer that. Best online mattress typically have a lifespan of about ten years, so if you've had your mattress for ten years or longer, it's probably time to upgrade. Next, if you noticed physical signs of wear and tear on your mattress, like it's sinking in or sagging, it's probably lived its life to the fullest and it's time to go. Well, unfortunately, there is not one Goto magical mattress for everyone. I really wish there was, but it all comes down to your different body type, the way you sleep, and just your personal characteristics. So I'm going to first start out by going over the different types of mattresses, starting with Inner spring. The most traditional mattress style on the market is the Inner Spring mattress. As the name States, they have Springs in them. They've been around forever. Well, not cave days forever, but in the mattress world forever. They're popular because they're supportive, durable, bouncy, and firm. They're especially nice for people who like to feel on top of the bed rather than in it. And they tend to sleep cool, then we have memory foam mattresses. Memory foam is known for its sinkage and body contouring. It provides great pressure relief, which is nice for sleepers looking for cushiony support at the shoulders and hips. This can also be great for people who suffer from shoulder, hip and back pain. Sleepers are going to feel more in the bed than on top of it with a memory foam mattress. And side note, there are a lot of different types of foams out there, like latex memory cooling gel and more. You can find more about them and what they do@sleepopolis.com. Then we have the best of both worlds. You could say hybrid mattresses hybrids are extremely popular because they combine Springs and foam. They can be bouncy and supportive while also giving you that contouring and pressure relief you need. They also tend to be very durable and cooling. Now that we've covered the types of mattresses, let's go over the different sizes. Mattresses come in the sizes of Twin, Twin, Excel, Full, Queen, King, Split King, and California King. Twin is the smallest size at 38 X 75. This is great for kids or small adults who aren't sharing the bets. Then we have Twin XL, which is 38 X 80. This is the same width as a Twin, but it's longer in length, so that's where the XL comes in. College dorms typically have Twin XLS for reference, so they're good for taller adults and kids. Folds are where we start to get wider in size at 54 X 75, so they allow more room for adults and could work for some couples who don't mind squeezing in together. Queen is going to be your most popular size at 60 X 80. They work for most couples and single adults. King Size is as wide as they come at 76 X 80, so they're good for couples who want to spread out more and not be up in each other's business too much in the night or people who share the bed with their pets. I have no shame in allowing pets to sleep in my bed, but I know everyone has their own opinion. A splitting is good for couples who want to have different mattress preferences and customize their feel. It's two sets of 38 X 80. You have your own side and your own bed with that. And finally, we have California King at 72 X 84. These are not quite as wide as a King, but they are longer than Kings, so this is good for you tall folks out there. And really, any couples or people with pets. Okay, now let's talk about sleeping position, which is an extremely important component when it comes to picking a new mattress. I want you to think about how you spend the majority of your time when you are sleeping at night. Are you on your back, your side or your stomach, perhaps even a combination of two or all of them? Personally, I sleep on my side and my back, and it helps me know what kind of mattress I need. Basically, it all comes down to pressure relief and keeping your spine in alignment, so let's go through them all. Starting with back sleepers. Back sleepers are the easiest to please. They need a mattress that's supportive and comfortable, that will allow their hips to sink in a little and keep their spine in alignment. Honestly, most beds I test work well for these sleepers, depending if you have any soreness or aches, you'll likely want a softer mattress or a mattress with foam, though. Side sleepers pretty much always want a softer mattress because they need their hips and shoulders to be able to sink into the bed the right amount and give them pressure relief at those points while also keeping their spines in alignment. Stomach sleepers, on the other hand, want a firm mattress. That's because they need their hips to be elevated for spinal alignment. It's not about pressure relief for them, but just about not letting their hips sink in. And when I say soft or firm, I'm referring to a firmness scale that goes from one to ten, with six and a half being the standard for medium firm. So stomach sleepers typically will want something above that six and a half, while side sleepers will want something below it. Back sleepers are right around it and can really be happy on pretty much anything. But I do want to point out that foam mattress buying be firm, just as they can be soft. And the same goes for hybrid and inner spring mattresses. How much you weigh is also going to be an important factor. We categorize lightweight sleepers as being under £130, average weight 130 to £250, and heavy weight is over £250. Light sleepers are likely going to find firm mattresses firmer than average weight sleepers. On the other hand, heavyweight sleepers are likely going to find mattresses a bit softer than the average sleeper. It's important to keep this in mind when looking for a mattress. And also, if you're a heavyweight sleeper, you're going to want something that's supportive and likely have inner Springs. We've picked some great options for you to look at as a start. If you share the bed with a partner, you're going to want to look for some additional factors. Edge support is one of them. Edge support is how the perimeter of a bed supports weight, so if you're laying near the edge of the bed, you shouldn't feel like it's about to collapse under you. Edge support is also a good sign of a mattress's durability and whether there's any chance of sagging over time. If a mattress has structured or reinforced edges, then that's a good sign of it. Motion isolation is something else that couples are going to want to keep in mind. This is how movement travels across the bed, so how likely you are to feel your partner tossing and turning on the other side of the mattress, typically memory foam mattress. Buying hybrid mattresses with thick layers of foam on top do the best at isolating motion cooling is another one. And hey, that's not just for couples. I tend to sleep hot myself and always love a good cooling mattress, but two people tend to overheat more than one. So if you're looking for a bed that's going to be cooling, you're going to want to look for cooling properties like whether it has cooling gel, phase change material, or inner spring. Hybrid mattresses and inner spring mattresses tend to be the most cooling, but I've personally tested some all foam mattresses that have really strong cooling properties. Other characteristics I often hear people look for in a bed, or whether it's eco friendly, if it's made in the USA or if it's compatible with an adjustable frame. So again, just different details you can keep in mind and see what's important to you when you're looking for a new mattress. Well, if you're going to shop in person, I recommend going in knowing what you want and having a price range in mind. - Mattress Buying Guide | Consumer Reports: Kind: captions Language: en Mattress companies will sell you the promise of a perfect night's sleep, sometimes for thousands of dollars. Their offerings-- hybrid mattresses, cooling gels, and customized sleep settings-- can be confusing to navigate. To help you pick, Consumer Reports puts mattresses to the test. This video is interactive, so click any chapter to skip around. [MUSIC PLAYING] We determine how well a mattress will support your spine, whether you sleep on your side or your back. We simulate eight years of use to see how a mattress keeps its shape, firmness, and support. We even cut mattresses open to see what's inside. This guide will arm you with expert knowledge to pick the style or model that best suits your needs. Consumer Reports' online subscribers can find specific brand and retailer recommendations as well as product ratings on our website. While you can find mattresses that use all sorts of materials, from foams and latex to mostly air, almost half of those sold are the classic inner spring. The springs are actually metal, usually steel coils. In some models, those coils are individually wrapped. Others are coils within coils. And some even have an hourglass look to them. Many inner spring manufacturers are offering hybrid models, adding several inches of cushioning, foam or padding, and gel-infused foam layers above the inner springs. Our tests showed none of these add-ons truly sets one inner spring mattress apart from another. One way we figure this out is by measuring the natural curve of a person's spine and then monitoring whether those curves are supported on a mattress. Our tests do find that inner springs overall are a bit better than other types of mattresses for side sleepers. Often, inner springs are easier to move around, compared to memory foam mattresses, because they offer more bounce. But there's a downside. Some models can transmit bounciness. So if you've got a restless co-sleeper, you'll feel it. We drop a weighted ball on each mattress to see how well it resists that bounciness. You can get a sense of this when you shop in a store by lying down with your partner and checking whether you feel his or her movements. Memory foam mattresses are steadily gaining in popularity. They make up 19% of mattress sales. And a recent Consumer Reports survey of almost 62,000 subscribers found 75% of memory foam mattress owners were completely or very satisfied with their choice. Memory foam is mostly polyurethane, a durable plastic substance that response to the heat and pressure of your body and is supposed to conform to it. Some memory foam beds offer dual firmness. That way, you and your partner can customize how firm or soft you want your side. Memory foam also does a great job of isolating movement so you won't get jostled when your partner tosses and turns on his or her sleep. But changing positions on some memory foam requires effort, because you tend to think into the mattress. Some people also say it sleeps hot. Many manufacturers are infusing the foam with gel that they claim will reduce surface temperature. One of the major downsides of some memory foam mattresses is the plasticky chemical smell. That's likely the polyurethane-based memory foam. But other chemicals, like flame retardants and fillers, which can be used in foam and other beds, could also lead to obnoxious odors. Consumer Reports says be sure to error out any memory foam mattress for at least 24 to 48 hours before you cover it with sheets and sleep on it. If you're worried about the smell but still want the memory foam feel, you might want to opt for natural latex. Natural latex is made using serum from rubber trees that's processed using one of two methods whose names you may hear-- Dunlop and Talalay. Dunlop produces a firmer material, while the more expensive Talalay yields a softer mattress. Many manufacturers will layer a mattress using both processes to create a customized feel. There are also synthetic latex mattresses. These tend to be less expensive than natural latex but are blended with other chemicals. Adjustable air mattresses allow you to control the firmness. No, we're not talking about the plastic inflatable ones you throw on the floor for guests or camping. These are mattresses filled with pockets of air, called air bladders, surrounded by foam. Many come with other layers of material, like foam or gel tops, for added comfort. In our survey of almost 62,000 subscribers, people with arthritis, back pain, or neck pain are most satisfied sleeping on an adjustable air mattress. Most adjustable air mattresses allow you to customize half the bed to your desired level of firmness anytime you want with a remote control, usually right from the bed. However, some people find the foam support that divides the two sides uncomfortable to roll over, so think about this if you tend to move around a lot. Try adjusting the firmness up and down at the store. You want it to respond quickly and relatively quietly. More companies are selling mattresses marketed as green, natural, or organic, partly in response to consumers' questions about chemicals in mattresses. For example, there are concerns with the use of certain fire-retardant chemicals-- in particular, polybrominated diphenyl ethers, known as PBDEs. PBDEs have been banned in some states, Canada, and Europe over health issues. Newer flame retardants have replaced the use of some PBDEs, but there are worries with those, as well. Some mattresses can also contain formaldehyde and other fuming chemicals from certain adhesives, the materials used in mattress construction. And harmful pesticides can be used to grow conventional cotton. But natural and organic may not mean what you think. The term "natural" isn't regulated by the government or any third party, so it's meaningless. And a mattress can be labeled organic even if only a portion of its materials are certified organic. Consumer Reports says there are two meaningful third-party certifications to look for-- the Global Organic Textile Standard, or GOTS, and the Global Organic Latex Standard, or GOLS. GOTS requires an organic mattress contain at least 95% certified organic fiber. And it prohibits the use of harmful processing materials and chemicals, allergenic dyes, and many other potentially harmful ingredients. The GOLS label requires that any latex in a mattress come from a certified organic source. Natural latex mattresses may have both the GOLS and GOTS label. There are other labels that can help lessen your exposure to certain chemicals. The Oeko-Tex Standard 100, the Greenguard Gold, and the Greenguard labels all test final products for potentially harmful chemical emissions and set limits on volatile organic compounds, or VOCs. The Oeko-Tex takes it a step further and bans the use of certain flame retardants and dyes containing carcinogens and allergens. If you're concerned about chemicals added to polyurethane, look for the CertiPUR-US label. It forbids the use of and requires testing for other chemical emissions, such as formaldehyde in polyurethane foam. Prices for mattresses with green claims run from as little as $600 to more than $25,000 for luxury versions. In general, if you're looking for certification, expect to pay around $2,000 or more for a queen-sized mattress. Our Consumer Reports survey data shows people typically spend about $500 to $1,750 on a new mattress. And those who successfully haggled over the price saved a median of around $200. There are upscale brands charging $30,000 to more than $100,000 for luxury materials like horse hair and cashmere. But our tests find you can buy a perfectly good mattress for under $1,000. The sweet spot? It's about $600 to $1,500. And with more online-only mattress retailers springing up, pricing is getting more competitive. We tested many mattresses sold on the internet and recommend several. Consumer Reports says look for companies that offer at least a 30-night trial, free shipping on returns, and full credit on that return. Generally, online manufacturers don't rely on sales, so the advertised price is what you'll pay. However, at a physical store, aim to pay 50% or more off of the regular price. That's sometimes called the MSRP, or Manufacturer's Suggested Retail Price. Stores usually list these prices right along with the sale prices. Skip extended warranties. They usually only cover manufacturing defects, not normal wear and tear. says save your money and buy new sheets instead. When it's time to give a mattress a test drive, don't let sexy sales terms like "ultra plush," "super soft," or even "firm" sway you. Consumer Reports independently measures firmness by applying a slow, steady force to each mattress. The mattress' response to that force is recorded, averaged, and assigned a value from 1 through 10. 10 represents a very firm mattress, and 1 is very soft. Our tests consistently find differences between what manufacturers claim and our results. So concentrate on whether it feels right to you. A few tips. First, choose the firmest mattress that's still comfortable. You want your mattress to support your spine but still allow it to maintain its natural curve. Next, bring your pillow from home. When lying on your back, make sure the mattress meets the curve of your lower back. On your side, you don't want your shoulders and hips taking on too much pressure or sinking in too far, which can throw your spine into an uncomfortable shape. Try to roll into a different position and note whether it's easy. After lying on the bed for at least 15 minutes, if you feel pain at your heaviest points, the mattress may be too firm. Aches, numbness, or tingling mean you're on a mattress that's too soft. Here are common sizes you'll see. The largest mattress is the king, at 76 by 80 inches. California king's aren't as wide as kings, but they are longer-- 72 by 84 inches. A queen is 60 by 80 inches. Fuller double beds are 53 by 75 inches, and twins, 38 by 75 inches. Many companies will craft a custom size, too-- for a price. Now that you've got the basics, visit ConsumerReports.org to subscribe and find our specific mattress brand ratings based on our in-depth expert testing. We're a not-for-profit, and we pay for all of the items we. test.
